for Hacker News\n\nHarmonic for Hacker News is an Android Hacker News client which is meant to be modern, fast and well thought out. You can find it on Google Play for download.\n\nThis has been my personal side project since 2020 and since starting my PhD in 2021 the time I have to dedicate to Harmonic has decreased. My personal philosophy on side projects is that the most important thing is building something that is actually shipped. Since much of my knowledge on building Android apps is ~10 years old, Harmonic does not use Kotlin or the latest Android API\'s and is probably not the easiest app to dig into the source code of. However, it is shipped, and it works fairly well in my opinion at least. :)\n\nThe purpose of open-sourcing Harmonic is thus mainly to enable interested users to submit pull requests fixing issues or adding features. I would like to still be able to find my way around the app\'s code so I am not looking for a full Kotlin rewrite but if you have something you\'d like to see fixed or added I am  very open to PR\'s. for Hacker News\n\nHarmonic for Hacker News is an Android Hacker News client which is meant to be modern, fast and well thought out. You can find it on Google Play for download.\n\nThis has been my personal side project since 2020 and since starting my PhD in 2021 the time I have to dedicate to Harmonic has decreased. My personal philosophy on side projects is that the most important thing is building something that is actually shipped. Since much of my knowledge on building Android apps is ~10 years old, Harmonic does not use Kotlin or the latest Android API\'s and is probably not the easiest app to dig into the source code of. However, it is shipped, and it works fairly well in my opinion at least. :)\n\nThe purpose of open-sourcing Harmonic is thus mainly to enable interested users to submit pull requests fixing issues or adding features. I would like to still be able to find my way around the app\'s code so I am not looking for a full Kotlin rewrite but if you have something you\'d like to see fixed or added I am  very open to PR\'s. I\'ll still commit things myself when I have time.\n\n<a href="https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.simon.harmonichackernews">\n<img src="https://play.google.com/intl/en_us/badges/images/generic/en_badge_web_generic.png" alt="Get it on Google Play" height="80">\n</a>\n\n# Features\n\n* Basic account functionality: log in, vote, comment, and submit\n* Material 3 design with some nice animations\n* Several themes including full black\n* A ton of customization options, bordering on feature creep\n* Fast!', 'stderr': '', 'interrupted': False, 'isImage': False, 'noOutputExpected': False})

getTimeAgo(long time) {\n        if (time < 1000000000000L) {\n            // if timestamp given in seconds, convert to millis\n            time *= 1000;\n        }\n\n        long now = System.currentTimeMillis();\n        if (time > now || time <= 0) {\n            return "?";\n        }\n\n        final long diff = now - time;\n        if (diff < MINUTE_MILLIS) {\n * HOUR_MILLIS) {\n            return "1 day";\n        } else if (diff < 365 * DAY_MILLIS) {\n            return diff / DAY_MILLIS + " days";\n        } else if (diff < 2 * YEAR_MILLIS) {\n getTimeAgo(long time) {\n        if (time < 1000000000000L) {\n            // if timestamp given in seconds, convert to millis\n            time *= 1000;\n        }\n\n        long now = System.currentTimeMillis();\n        if (time > now || time <= 0) {\n            return "?";\n        }\n\n        final long diff = now - time;\n        if (diff < MINUTE_MILLIS) {\n Intent(Intent.ACTION_VIEW, Uri.parse(url));\n            ctx.startActivity(browserIntent);\n        } catch (Exception e) {\n            // failed for the first time, let\'s try to guess a fix to the url\n            try {\n                Intent browserIntent = new Intent(Intent.ACTION_VIEW, Uri.parse(URLUtil.guessUrl(url)));\n                ctx.startActivity(browserIntent);\n            } catch (Exception e1) {\n                // automated fix didn\'t work, let\'s try to do it manually\n                try {\n                    if (!url.startsWith("http://") && ctx.startActivity(browserIntent);\n                } catch (Exception e2) {\n                    Toast.makeText(ctx, "Couldn\'t open link to: " + url, Toast.LENGTH_SHORT).show();\n                }\n public static ArrayList<ResolveInfo> getCustomTabsPackages(Context context) {\n        PackageManager pm = context.getPackageManager();\n        // Get default VIEW intent handler.\n        Intent initialTime} is after {@code finalTime}, then {@code currentTime} must be between\n     * last day\'s {@code initialTime} and this day\'s {@code finalTime} or this day\'s {@code initialTime}\n     * and next day\'s {@code finalTime}\n     */\n    public static boolean isTimeBetweenTwoTimes(long initialTime, long finalTime, long currentTime) {\n        if (finalTime < initialTime) {\n            finalTime += TimeUnit.DAYS.toMinutes(1);\n        }\n\n        if (currentTime < initialTime) {\n            currentTime += TimeUnit.DAYS.toMinutes(1);\n        }\n\n        return initialTime <= res.getBoolean(R.bool.is_tablet);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Hacker News truncates the visible text of long links in comments, keeping only a prefix of\n     * the url and appending an ellipsis, while the {@code href} attribute keeps the full url. That\n     * is fine when simply reading a comment, but it means the full link cannot be selected or\n     * copied. This replaces such truncated link texts with the complete url from the href.\n     * <p>\n     * Link texts which are not just a shortened version of their href (regular, descriptive link\n     * texts) are left untouched.\n     */\n    public static String expandTruncatedLinks(String html) {\n        if (TextUtils.isEmpty(html)) {\n            return html;\n        }\n\n        Matcher matcher = A_TAG_PATTERN.matcher(html);\n        StringBuffer result = new StringBuffer();\n\n        while (matcher.find()) {\n            String openingTag = matcher.group(1);\n            String href = matcher.group(2);\n            String linkText = matcher.group(3);\n            String closingTag = matcher.group(4);\n\n            String expandedText = isTruncatedVersionOf(linkText, href) ? href : linkText;\n\n            matcher.appendReplacement(result,\n                    Matcher.quoteReplacement(openingTag + expandedText + closingTag));\n        }\n        matcher.appendTail(result);\n\n        return result.toString();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Whether {@code linkText} looks like an ellipsized version of {@code href}, i.e. a prefix of\n     * the url followed by an ellipsis. Both are compared in their html escaped form, which is\n     * consistent since they originate from the same document.\n     */\n    private static boolean isTruncatedVersionOf(String linkText, String href) {\n        if (TextUtils.isEmpty(linkText) || TextUtils.isEmpty(href)) {\n            return false;\n        }\n\n        String prefix = null;\n        for (String ellipsis : ELLIPSES) {\n            if (linkText.endsWith(ellipsis)) {\n                prefix = linkText.substring(0, linkText.length() - ellipsis.length());\n                break;\n            }\n        }\n\n        if The build succeeds and the bug is fixed.\n\n## Diagnosis\n\nThe truncation is **not** done by the app or the vendored `HtmlTextView` library — I verified that `HtmlTagHandler` renders anchor text verbatim, and found no `ellipsize`/`substring` logic on the comment body. It comes from **Hacker News\' own server-side HTML**, which shortens the *visible* text of long links while keeping the full URL in the `href`:\n\n```html\n<a href="https://www.federalreserve.gov/monetarypolicy/files/monetary20240101a1.pdf"\n   rel="nofollow">https://www.federalreserve.gov/monetarypolicy/files/monetar...</a>\n```\n\n`DialogUtils.showTextSelectionDialog()` passed that raw HTML straight to `setHtml(text)`. So the select-text view inherited HN\'s truncation — and since selection operates on rendered text, selecting a link could never yield more than the `...`-terminated prefix. The full URL was sitting right there in the `href`, just never rendered.\n\n## Fix\n\nAdded `Utils.expandTruncatedLinks(String html)`, which rewrites each anchor\'s inner text with its full `href`, and called it from the select-text dialog.\n\nThe key design concern was **not clobbering legitimate link text**. A blanket "replace all anchor text with the href" would turn `<a href="...">click here</a>` into a raw URL. So `isTruncatedVersionOf()` only substitutes when the text is genuinely an ellipsized form of its own href — the text ends in `...` (or `…`) *and* the remaining prefix actually prefixes the href. Descriptive text, and prose that merely trails off with `...`, is left alone.\n\nDetails worth noting:\n- **Escaped entities**: text and href are compared in their HTML-escaped form. That\'s sound because both come from the same document and are unescaped identically downstream, so `&amp;` in a query string matches correctly.\n- **`Matcher.quoteReplacement`**: without it, a `$` or `\\` in a URL would corrupt `appendReplacement`.\n- **Scheme-less display text** is handled, since HN sometimes omits `https://`.\n- Anything ambiguous falls through and is left untouched — a no-op, never corruption.\n\nI validated this against 13 cases in a standalone harness (real HN-style links, descriptive text, prose ellipsis, multiple mixed links per comment, escaped ampersands, `$`/`\\` in URLs, unicode ellipsis, null/empty) before building.\n\nTwo notes on scope:\n- I deliberately kept the change to the select-text view, matching the report. It comes from **Hacker News\' own server-side HTML**, which shortens the *visible* text of long links while keeping the full URL in the `href`:\n\n```html\n<a href="https://www.federalreserve.gov/monetarypolicy/files/monetary20240101a1.pdf"\n   rel="nofollow">https://www.federalreserve.gov/monetarypolicy/files/monetar...</a>\n```\n\n`DialogUtils.showTextSelectionDialog()` passed that raw HTML straight to `setHtml(text)`. So the select-text view inherited HN\'s truncation — and since selection operates on rendered text, selecting a link could never yield more than the `...`-terminated prefix. The full URL was sitting right there in the `href`, just never rendered.\n\n## Fix\n\nAdded `Utils.expandTruncatedLinks(String html)`, which rewrites each anchor\'s inner text with its full `href`, and called it from the select-text dialog.\n\nThe key design concern was **not clobbering legitimate link text**. A blanket "replace all anchor text with the href" would turn `<a href="...">click here</a>` into a raw URL. So `isTruncatedVersionOf()` only substitutes when the text is genuinely an ellipsized form of its own href — the text ends in `...` (or `…`) *and* the remaining prefix actually prefixes the href. Descriptive text, and prose that merely trails off with `...`, is left alone.\n\nDetails worth noting:\n- **Escaped entities**: text and href are compared in their HTML-escaped form. That\'s sound because both come from the same document and are unescaped identically downstream, so `&amp;` in a query string matches correctly.\n- **`Matcher.quoteReplacement`**: without it, a `$` or `\\` in a URL would corrupt `appendReplacement`.\n- **Scheme-less display text** is handled, since HN sometimes omits `https://`.\n- Anything ambiguous falls through and is left untouched — a no-op, never corruption.\n\nI validated this against 13 cases in a standalone harness (real HN-style links, descriptive text, prose ellipsis, multiple mixed links per comment, escaped ampersands, `$`/`\\` in URLs, unicode ellipsis, null/empty) before building.\n\nTwo notes on scope:\n- I deliberately kept the change to the select-text view, matching the report. The adjacent **"copy text"** action in `CommentsFragment` (line 1600) does `Html.fromHtml(comment.text)` and has the *same* underlying defect — copied links are truncated too. It\'s a one-line reuse of the new helper if you want it fixed, but I left it out rather than widen scope unasked.\n- The normal comment list still shows HN\'s truncated links, which I understood to be intended for layout reasons per the report\'s framing.\n\nThe patch is saved at `bug-171_fix.patch` (this directory is under the research repo\'s gitignored `runs/`, so there was no VCS baseline — I reconstructed the originals to produce a proper unified diff).', structured_output=None, model_usage={'claude-haiku-4-5-20251001': {'inputTokens': 828, 'outputTokens': 17, 'cacheReadInputTokens': 0, 'cacheCreationInputTokens': 0, 'webSearchRequests': 0, 'costUSD': 0.000913, 'contextWindow': 200000, 'maxOutputTokens': 32000}, 'claude-opus-5': {'inputTokens': 2875, 'outputTokens': 16684, 'cacheReadInputTokens': 912120, 'cacheCreationInputTokens': 42854, 'webSearchRequests': 0, 'costUSD': 1.1553725000000001, 'contextWindow': 200000, 'maxOutputTokens': 32000}}, permission_denials=[], deferred_tool_use=None, errors=None, api_error_status=None, uuid='253557cf-3e20-48a5-90d5-93acf6992055')
